ParlaMint-FR_2018-11-13-O1061.u1	The next item is the continuation of the discussion of the second part of the 2019 Finance Bill (No. 1255, 1302).
ParlaMint-FR_2018-11-13-O1061.u2	This afternoon, the Assembly began the examination of credits for school education (No. 1302, annex 24; No 1303, Volume II), as well as sports, youth and associative life (No 1302, Annex 42; No 1303, Volume VI), with reference to Amendment No 1785 to Article 39 (State B). Mr Aurélien Pradié has the floor to support Amendment No. 1785.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-11-13-O1061.u3	I need some explanations such as, I think, many colleagues here – including, I suppose, colleagues from the majority, who are very attentive to budgetary debates. In 2018, you allocated 12,911 million euros for the training of AESHs – accompanying students with disabilities –, AVSs – school life assistants – and assisted contracts. In the draft budget for 2019 which is proposed to us, on the same line, under exactly the same heading – "Training of AESH, AVS and assisted contracts" – you propose to allocate 7,069 million euros. This leads to a reduction of more than €5 million in this training line, a bleed of almost 42%. This is all the more regrettable because already in 2018, the funds allocated to the training of the AESH, AVS and assisted contracts were not enough, which everyone can agree – I know you can, sometimes, show some good faith. As you announce the creation of 4500 new accompanying posts – in the general sense of the term – you are reducing the training budget of these people by 42%. I therefore need explanations, Mr Minister of National Education and Youth, like, I think, all Members in good faith.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-11-13-O1061.u4	Mrs Catherine Osson, Special Rapporteur of the Committee on Finance, the General Economy and Budgetary Control, has the floor to give the Committee's opinion.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-11-13-O1061.u5	Mr Pradié, you want to increase by 8.6 million the appropriations allocated for the training of HESAs and aided contracts. This training is essential for the serene support of children with disabilities and for dealing with all situations that may arise on a daily basis. I share your willingness to provide funding to ensure quality training. I would point out to you, in this regard, because I know that you appreciate the precise arguments, that the decree of 27 July 2018 on the conditions of recruitment and employment of accompanying students with disabilities has set the minimum duration of training for these persons at 60 hours, in order to secure their situation. With regard to the reduction of appropriations, this is in fact an effort of budgetary sincerity, since the envelope has not been fully consumed in implementation. In addition, this envelope is only indicative and will be adjusted in the course of the year according to needs. I therefore ask you to withdraw your amendment; Otherwise, my opinion would be, on a personal basis, unfavourable, since the committee did not consider this amendment.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-11-13-O1061.u6	The Minister for National Education and Youth has the floor to give the Government's opinion.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-11-13-O1061.u7	Same opinion.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-11-13-O1061.u8	Mr Aurélien Pradié has the floor.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-11-13-O1061.u9	Madam Special Rapporteur, I regret to say that you did not provide me with the details I expected. I'm trying to figure out what you're telling us. You state that this is an effort of budgetary sincerity, that the envelope for 2018 has not been completely consumed. However, you know as I do that the conditions under which training last year was provided were not satisfactory – everyone will tell you, including perfectly politically neutral organizations. If the envelope has not been exhausted, this can be explained by the fact that the training offered did not meet the needs. So you cannot claim to reduce the credits to adapt them to the needs expressed last year, even though they were largely undervalued and inappropriate training. The difference this year – you remind her often enough to have it in mind, I hope – is that you create 4500 additional accompanying positions. How, under these conditions, can you justify the 42% decrease in the 2018 envelope? I'm sorry, minister, but you can't permanently tell that the formation of AVS, AESH and assisted contracts is your priority, saying – you're right to say it – that it's one of the strategic topics, and giving yourself a historic bleed – 42% – of the budget dedicated to it. ParlaMint-FR_2018-11-13-O1061.u10	Mr Gilles Lurton has the floor.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-11-13-O1061.u11	Minister, I would also like to get some explanations, without any polemics. You are going to transform school life assistants into AESH, which will now follow disabled children in schools. They will have a level of study corresponding to the baccalaureate and will be eligible for training, once they are recruited by national education under long-term contracts. This seems satisfactory to me, because this was not the case with the AVS. However, the AVS that were previously in the position do not, for many of them, have the level of the baccalaureate. Will they be able to claim to become AESH? If not, what will they become after the transformation? Will it simply put an end to their contract? Thank you for telling us what their situation will be.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-11-13-O1061.u12	The Minister has the floor.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-11-13-O1061.u13	The issue of the formation of the AESH is, as everyone has said, extremely important, and we must live up to this requirement. Mr Pradieu, I would like to reassure you. Perhaps you have in mind the staffing that was spent on the AESH training in 2017?
ParlaMint-FR_2018-11-13-O1061.u14	The problem isn't here!
ParlaMint-FR_2018-11-13-O1061.u15	It amounted to €2.9 million, while we propose €7.3 million for 2019. So, as you can see, this is an increase which, over two years, is well over 100%. In fact, last year, our programming – worth more than €12 million – proved excessive. This year, we are making an effort of budgetary sincerity, leading to the posting of 7.6 million. However, as the Special Rapporteur has said very well, we are prepared to abound this envelope in the course of the year if it appears insufficient to achieve the ambitious objective we have set ourselves: to provide all the AESHs with an annual training of 60 hours. There is no point in portraying in regression what constitutes enormous progress. I do not see our interest in playing this game in front of the public concerned. The reality, expressed without fail, is that in two years – I insist on the figures – we will go from 2.9 million to 7.6 million, and this last figure, moreover, is not frozen. Mr Lurton, we are indeed willing to give the ex-AVS the status of AESH, which leads us to implement policies to validate the experience gained and to relax the recruitment of these accompanying persons, from the beginning of this year. This policy will continue, and I again propose that we cooperate on this. Until February, we will work with the representative associations to consolidate this status and, in particular, allow the AESHs to work full-time – if they are poorly paid, it is often due to the fact that they work part-time. To this end, we envisage that they will exercise their activity both on school time and on extracurricular time. We therefore want to introduce this systematic training of 60 hours, and to give it more qualitative strength, so that, at the beginning of 2019, this budget allows us to have 11,000 additional AESH, well-trained and with more stable prospects. These are qualitative issues that should help to unite national representation, in order to move forward in the direction that everyone calls for.

However, the gap between singing and the feelings of both students and adults is immense. Music teachers face enormous difficulties in making other music heard in classes with more than twenty-eight students, and are not always in the best position to defend such a requirement. This option, now open to every college in France, represents a budgetary cost of 20 million euros. In addition to the EUR 17 million included in the allocation to colleges, EUR 3 million is included in the budget of the Ministry of Culture. In reality, we are making an unprecedented effort to develop choirs, which we want to systematize in institutions – we are achieving this goal. We have created the Choir of National Education, made up of music teachers, at the national level, as well as choirs in each academy. In addition, we have developed various tools, including digital tools. The "Music Prime" portal, managed by the Ministry of National Education, has been renewed and now offers all the necessary resources to choir leaders. In partnership with Radio France and Arte, we launched Vox, a mobile phone application to train teachers as choir conductors. Thus, this amendment deals with a magnificent subject of development of artistic and cultural education, which translates qualitatively and budgetaryly. ParlaMint-FR_2018-11-13-O1061.u145	What is the opinion of the committee?

ParlaMint-FR_2018-11-13-O1061.u148	There are at least two dimensions in your question. We did not want to take hot action after an event which, alas, is not isolated: indeed, many violent acts have been committed in our school system for a long time and we must react in a calm and profound way. That is what we did by confirming actions started 18 months ago. I refer you to the measures that I announced about ten days ago, which aim to secure the class and the institution, including the monitoring of each fact and the implementation of a proportionate sanction, but also measures such as the strengthening of mobile security teams or the simplification of disciplinary boards. In addition, we have decided to conduct interdisciplinary and interdepartmental work on security issues in the vicinity of institutions, on the security ecosystem of institutions. This work requires cooperation with the ministries of justice, the interior and solidarity, but also with ministries such as those of culture or sport. As I said, we want to go in three directions: securing the surroundings of the establishments; the creation of structures dedicated to the most difficult pupils, in particular those called polyexcluded; Finally, a reflection on the responsibility of parents and how we bring parents and schools together on this issue. These initiatives are the subject of a working group of which MP Stéphane Testé and the mayor of Morlaix and Ms Béatrice Gille, president of the Montpellier Academy, are members. An Inspector General coordinates this work. As the President of the Republic has said, in the course of December we will announce the measures that will complement those already announced. We therefore ask you, Minister, to restore in 2019 the 2018 workforce level by returning to the abolition of these fifty positions.

ParlaMint-FR_2018-11-13-O1061.u213	What is the Government's opinion?
ParlaMint-FR_2018-11-13-O1061.u214	Madam Member, your question gives me the opportunity to address the subject of evaluation in the school system, which is obviously a very important subject. Our department, as you know, has several evaluation bodies: general inspections, which are the oldest; a direction dedicated to evaluation and foresight; Finally, CNESCO, which is the most recent body. As you know, I attach great importance to evaluation, because it is the lever for the evolution of any system. The debate is therefore not about the merits of the evaluation itself, since I think we are all in favour of it: it is about how to carry out this evaluation. You mentioned the situation in CNESCO. We will have an opportunity to talk more specifically about this when we look at this bill for the school of trust. In a few words, as I have already had the opportunity to say in committee, the aim is not to abolish CNESCO, but to strengthen its action, giving it a more appropriate form than its present form. Over the years, CNESCO has become an expert body on public policy, rather than an evaluation entity of schools and institutions in our school system. The aim is to take what is positive in the trajectory of CNESCO and to give it an even more academic and autonomous character in the future. Important developments are emerging. For example, we are in the process of merging the general inspections, which had been expected for many years. It is a decision that all the experts on the subject were waiting for and that we are in the process of achieving. It will be ripe in the middle of 2019. As the Special Rapporteur has just pointed out, we are really changing our entire evaluation system. In the next school law, we want to create a real body to evaluate the school system. We now have a quality expertise, but its fragmentary nature prevents it from having a real power of action, capable of changing the system. In addition, the budget for 2019 increases the means of evaluation by 1 million. But I repeat that, beyond the means, what matters most is our ability to organize ourselves, and that will be the object of the law to come. For all these reasons, unfavorable opinion.

ParlaMint-FR_2018-11-13-O1061.u219	Mrs Tamarelle-Verhaeghe has the floor to support Amendment No. 1935.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-11-13-O1061.u220	This amendment goes in the same direction and is primarily intended to shed light on this school medicine service, which is recognized as a valuable asset that we have been able to put at the service of our young people. It helps to preserve their health, but also to ensure their success, because one does not go without the other and it takes a certain well-being to succeed. The problem is known, and it is not recent. For a number of years, the High Council for Public Health, the Court of Auditors and the Economic, Social and Environmental Council have been pointing to a crisis in school medicine. This crisis is multifaceted. First, there is a pay-related problem of attractiveness, as school doctors are the most poorly paid public service physicians. The second problem is the ever-increasing number of missions entrusted to them. Finally, there is the question of coordination with other actors, be it the liberal world or the IMPs. These professionals, as we can see, face complex and multiple problems. However, they have a major role to play in ensuring certain essential tasks such as preventing harassment or welcoming and succeeding children with disabilities. The "School health" action in the PLF amounts to €500 million, which, reduced to the number of students – 12 million – represents €40 per student per year. Missions are still increasing, yet this ratio has not moved in ten years.

ParlaMint-FR_2018-11-13-O1061.u233	On Amendment No 1787, I have a request from the Group of the European People's Party for a public vote. The vote is announced in the National Assembly. Mr Aurélien Pradié has the floor to support Amendment No. 1787.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-11-13-O1061.u234	This amendment concerns the travel costs of AVS, AESH and assisted contracts. Last year, the sum of 1.1 million was intended to cover these travel costs. Earlier, Mr.Minister, you stated that the travel expenses of trained persons were covered in the context of the discussion of the previous amendment. That's not true. The budget of 1.1 million is specifically intended to cover these costs for trained persons, including those involved in several schools. Let us recall the context: there will be an additional 4,500 AESH posts next year, which will automatically lead to an increase in the costs associated with these staff, including travel costs. Moreover, these staff are increasingly involved in different institutions. This trend has been true for at least two years. In spite of this, you have a budget that is strictly the same as last year's. This amendment tends to take into account the increase in the wage bill, so that these staff have the necessary means to move and train. Otherwise, these jobs will become more and more precarious, and training can be abandoned in the course of the year. This question may seem annoyed, but it is essential in the approach that seems to be yours to move towards "solid" jobs, as you describe them.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-11-13-O1061.u235	What is the opinion of the committee?
ParlaMint-FR_2018-11-13-O1061.u236	Unfavorable opinion.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-11-13-O1061.u237	What is the Government's opinion?
ParlaMint-FR_2018-11-13-O1061.u238	Two lines are planned, one concerning training costs and travel expenses of trainers, and the other concerning training AESHs. I will not repeat the explanations I gave earlier, but I undertake to explain these figures in a forthcoming document, which will probably be available on our website, which will help to remove any ambiguity. The issue of training is very internalised by national education. In 2018, we launched the localized inclusive support hubs – PIAL – which allow us to visualize the reception of students with disabilities and the management of human resources, in particular the AESHs and assisted contracts, since the school. We thus give the institution the means to assign staff according to the situation found, to approach the realities of the field as closely as possible and to design training actions at best. One of the virtuous effects of such an approach, which is adopted by the countries that achieve the best results in the field of reception of pupils with disabilities, is to stabilize posts, reducing travel, in particular to attend training courses, which can be offered within the institutions themselves. However, we do not reduce the appropriations to cover travel costs. We stabilize them. We will change them in the next budget, for 2020, if necessary, but for the moment they should be sufficient to cover the expenditure, especially since they are slightly higher than last year.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-11-13-O1061.u239	Mr Aurélien Pradié has the floor.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-11-13-O1061.u240	Thank you for these elements, minister, but you are not stabilizing the means since, since last year, even if you seem to have forgotten it, you have planned to hire an additional 4,500 staff. See, I learned the lesson! The stabilization of the budget will therefore result in a decrease in appropriations per staff. This amendment simply seeks to take account of the increase in the wage bill. It's still not an exorbitant demand! Since you have selected the 2017 benchmark, Minister, I have decided to rely on the 2017 and 2018 consumption benchmark. In the 2017 budget, €1076355 was credited for the same travel expenses. Furent consumed 1272511 euros: that's more than the 1.1 million you're offering us this year! But what happened in 2017, then this year? Twice, you have increased the number of staff by 4500! That's 9000 extra staff! Compared to the benchmark you selected yourself, in 2017, you are therefore reducing the funds allocated to travel, training and travel between multiple institutions. This is not a stabilisation, minister, but a significant reduction in resources.

ParlaMint-FR_2018-11-13-O1061.u242	Mr. Pradie spoke of "strong" jobs. Although this expression has been consecrated by the Minister, it does not exist in labour law. Since they work in national education, the least they can do would be to give them a status and to integrate them into a profession in national education. Let's banish this robust term once and for all. By the way, let us remember the importance of completing the training before taking up office! However, you do not address this subject. When an AESH learns, on the eve of the school year, that she will have to take care of an autistic child or another disability to which she has not been trained, you tell her that she will have to wait three months before receiving a suitable training! It's not serious. Finally, these staff would like to see the consultation meetings with the teaching team recorded in their service time, which is not the case today. It is better to include AESH in national education.

Your concern, Madam Special Rapporteur, is legitimate, since this new agency will be created by 1 March 2019. Without waiting for its regulatory formalization, we are now working to secure financial support for 2019 for federations, sports associations and local authorities. To this end, recruitment of the agency's prefigurator is under way; he will be appointed at the beginning of December 2018 and will work on the organisation of this future organization according to the guidelines already agreed. I'll give you an outline. For the high performance part, the grant campaign of the federations for 2019 will be conducted as follows: agreements will be concluded between the state and the federations, and renewed at the end of 2018 or in January 2019, in the light of the meetings with the federations conducted today by the "performance 2024" cell, led by Claude Onesta. These agreements will allow the State to make a first payment to the federations in the first half of 2019. As soon as the Agency is established, it will resume these agreements and amend them, in accordance with the guidelines laid down by its Management Board. The State's additional payments to the federations for 2019 will be made, potentially, on the basis of the amendments to these agreements. As regards the development of practices, the multi-annual agreements of objectives concluded with the federations will be extended or renewed by the Sports Directorate until the creation of the Agency in order, again, to make a first payment in the first quarter of 2019. The multi-annual operations for which the CNDS is responsible will, of course, be taken over by the Sport Agency. Upon the dissolution of the CNDS, the CNDS will take over all the rights and obligations relating to the agreements concluded to date. ParlaMint-FR_2018-11-13-O1061.u571	As you all know, the Buffet tax on television rights has been introduced to finance amateur sport and sport for the greatest number of people. However, the financing of sport through this tax is capped at EUR 25 million, with revenues exceeding this threshold being re-allocated to the general budget of the State. For example, in 2018, Buffet tax revenues amounted to more than 39 million euros, and nearly 14 million euros were reallocated to the State. This amount will be missed in the 2019 sports budget. The deflation of the tax is necessary, on the one hand, to contribute to the financing of the some 300,000 sports federations, which employ 100,000 employees and call on more than 3 million volunteers, on the other hand to develop sport in our territories. It is all the more necessary at this time that the French sports community must mobilize in preparation for the Olympic Games of 2024. With this amendment, I propose to draw up the missing appropriations from the action "Development of civic service" and to allocate them to the action "Promotion of sport for the greatest number" of the programme "Sport", bearing in mind that it is planned that the appropriations for the action "Development of civic service" will increase by 50 million euros in 2019 to more than 500 million, an amount almost twice as much as the total appropriations of the programme "Sport". For the future of our associations, it is important to defuse the Buffet tax.

The purpose of this amendment is, on the one hand, to require the payment of a financial contribution to private sports establishments which would hire state patent holders, and, on the other hand, which would be quite innovative, to create a label "Entreprise du sport français". Any company involved in the sport sector could benefit from this distinction, provided, inter alia, that it contributes to the financing of sports federations, which train those who will use its equipment and equipment during their careers.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-11-13-O1061.u636	What is the opinion of the committee?
ParlaMint-FR_2018-11-13-O1061.u637	Mr President, I understand the philosophy of your amendment, but I do not agree with it. Indeed, sports halls, unlike our sports associations, which are managed by the federations, are already subject to a 20% VAT rate. One can therefore have, in the same territory, an association and a private company competing in a given sport, which is not a simple situation. In addition, clubs often benefit from community-provided infrastructure, which is not the case for private companies. I therefore do not think it necessary to increase taxation on private companies. On the other hand, a professional athlete does not necessarily train in a federation for the exercise of his future profession: there are many other training bodies. We don't necessarily follow the training given by a federation to become, let's say, fitness coach. Therefore, it is not always the federation that trains high-performance athletes. This is why it does not seem necessary or appropriate to tax private companies and to give priority to federations which receive public aid.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-11-13-O1061.u638	What is the Government's opinion?
ParlaMint-FR_2018-11-13-O1061.u639	Some federations already incorporate private structures into their formations, such as the French tennis federation, which relies on private academies for the high level. In the same way, sports coaches work in private sports companies, either in addition to their activities in associations or to make part of their careers, before returning to federations, where they bring their experience. There is no philosophical reason to specifically tax these private companies, which employ state-trained sports educators.

ParlaMint-FR_2018-11-13-O1061.u734	We are dealing with the examination of the appropriations for the research and higher education mission (No 1302, Annexes 33 and 34; No 1303, Volumes IV and V; No 1288, Volume XIV; No 1285, Volume X). The Minister for Higher Education, Research and Innovation has the floor.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-11-13-O1061.u735	Keeping our position in international competition, playing a key role in elucidating major scientific issues, fostering innovation, better training and better graduating our students are the pillars of my commitment as Minister. The interdepartmental mission "Research and Higher Education" – MIRES – 2019 is the budgetary translation of this commitment carried out by the Government as a whole for more than 18 months. We've never invested as much in our future as we have now. Indeed, the budget that I have the honour to present to you has seen its appropriations increase by 5.3% in two years, or EUR 1.3 billion, in a financial context that everyone, in this House, measures. The objective set out in the public finance programming law of a MIRES exceeding EUR 28 billion by 2020 is, for the time being, respected. Next year, the budget of the Ministry of Higher Education, Research and Innovation – MESRI – will reach almost 25.1 billion euros, 549 million more than in the 2018 budget. On the full perimeter of MIRES, this increase is 500 million. In the area of higher education, 2019 will focus on continuing the reform of the first cycle and strengthening the autonomy of universities. A little over 123 million euros will finance, with the 150 programme, the personalized success paths and the compensatory measures that will support and value the commitment of staff in the implementation of Parcoursup as well as in educational innovation. In this respect, the future investment programme will continue to support the renovation of university curricula. This device, together with the provisions of the new license order, will allow to rethink the content of the first cycle in order to allow each student to build his or her course independently, by multiplying the bridges and the contact with several disciplines to gradually specialize. The reform of health studies will, of course, be part of the same spirit. For the first time this year, the regional centres of academic and academic works – CROUS – were able to collect the contribution for student and campus life, which will allow higher education institutions to offer students new health, sports and cultural services, while offering them a better reception. Some may have been concerned about the employment that would be made of these credits. I want to reassure you: a ceiling for the 2019-2020 school year has been set in finance law and will obviously be revised at the end of 2019, depending on the amount that will actually be collected by schools and universities next year. I would add that the Government has achieved a significant reduction in the cost of university re-entry, in particular by abolishing the €217 contribution to the student social security scheme and organizing the payment of grants on a date basis. Before the end of this year, we will publish the order on experimental groupings, in application of the provisions of the law for a state at the service of a trust society – ESSOC – in order to give each university site the opportunity to define its strategy, in terms of both international visibility and articulation with local territorial specificities. In addition to this order, I have been experimenting with a strategic management dialogue with nine universities since the beginning of the year, in order to exchange directly with them their needs, their projects and the developments of their own signature. As you know, last year, on the occasion of the 2018 Finance Act, we expanded the specialty principle to allow universities to better value their heritage. We will go further this year, with a second wave of devolutions, currently being finalized, which will concern the universities of Bordeaux, Marseille, Caen and Tours. Because it is just as priority as higher education, the research and innovation policy led by this government is also at the heart of the construction of MIRES 2019. In order to cope with international competition renewed by China's or India's announced voluntarism in this area, for example, my research priorities are simple: support for basic research, mobilization of scientific forces to respond to major societal challenges and strengthening partnerships between universities and research organizations. They are equally clear in terms of innovation: simplification is needed to enable the knowledge produced to express its potential for progress in the service of society. It is essential to strengthen the national missions of research organisations by entrusting them with priority research programmes at the national level, such as the programme for artificial intelligence led by the National Institute for Research in Computer Science and Automatics (INRIA) or the climate programme led by the National Centre for Scientific Research (CNRS). This will have to be done by encouraging the scientific excellence of tomorrow. As a result, 300 new PhD students will be recruited by CNRS to strengthen research laboratories operated jointly with universities. With regard to artificial intelligence, EUR 29 million from the MIRES and the investment programme for the future will finance the research component of the IA plan in 2019. This funding will grow year after year. In line with this ambition, the research budget will increase by 2.5% this year, an increase of 8% over two years. This effort underlines the strategic nature of the research for our country: 171 million for the 172 programme, which will amount to 6.9 billion euros, and 1.8 billion for the 193 programme, which will have an additional 205 million. I am convinced that project funding should not be opposed to basic funding: both must be done and done at the right level, regardless of the vector. That is why, in order to facilitate the work of researchers on a daily basis, I decided to renew in 2019 the direct funding "based" to laboratories to the amount of 25 million euros. The remuneration of EPST staff – public scientific and technological institutions – will also benefit from an increase of 28 million under the PPCR protocol – career paths, careers and remuneration. The recovery of the funding of the ANR – National Research Agency – will continue and this is reflected visibly in the budget: an additional 33 million in commitment authorities are registered in 2019, an increase of 65 million in two years. This corresponds to a firm commitment in terms of payment appropriations: 86 million in 2019, or 220 million more in two years. This is proof of the Government's long-term commitment to ensuring greater visibility and simplicity for our researchers. Support for virtuous initiatives, combining the best of private initiative and public research, will also be renewed. For example, in 2019, the €5 million effort already made in 2018 for the Carnot institutes, which are more than ever the levers of the rapprochement between research and industry, will also be renewed. After regularizing France's situation with international scientific organisations to the tune of 300 million euros in 2018, including more than 170 million outside the space sector, my ministry will remain fully committed in 2019 to funding both the very large research infrastructures and international organisations, as the budget will increase by 23 million. These are heavy and demanding but fundamental choices to push the frontier of knowledge. On this subject, as you know, Europe plays a fundamental role. This is both the natural geographical location of scientific and university cooperation, but also the place where tomorrow's university and research take shape. We must project our policies and practices at European level as part of the preparation of Horizon Europe, the world's first research programme, which is an opportunity for our country. That is why I have undertaken a specific project with this in mind. [[Applause on the benches of the LaREM and MODEM groups]] Because it was a commitment of the President of the Republic in his speech by the Sorbonne in 2017, I welcome the launch by the European Commission of the call for projects "European Universities", an indispensable milestone to make it clear that our common future is not conceivable outside the European framework. This EUR 30 million for six pilot projects will enable consortia of French and European establishments in at least three countries to build the first pilot projects. As part of this process, France will support at least 100 million projects over 10 years. Of course, all French institutions that wish to do so are called upon to take advantage of this opportunity, which will allow us to see, as early as 2019, the emergence of the first European universities.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-11-13-O1061.u736	Mr Fabrice Le Vigoureux, Special Rapporteur of the Committee on Finance, the General Economy and Budgetary Control, has the floor.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-11-13-O1061.u737	Mr President, Madam Minister, Mr President of the Committee on Finance, ladies and gentlemen, rapporteurs, ladies and gentlemen, dear insomniac viewers, before starting this budgetary sequence, I plunged myself into a remarkable novel by a young author, Marion Messina, which is called, in a symptomatic way, false departure. This work reflects the uneasiness and loneliness of a young student, Aurélie, facing this unknown continent that higher education can be in some families. The author describes in a Quebec Houelle style the weight of social determinisms that play full, not always for monetary reasons, and that lead Aurélie into a default academic path in which she is bored firm. This upset success, often very real, is for me a real citizen injury because talents are sometimes wasted, discouraged, and the culture of rebound is still to be established in France. As special rapporteur, university professor and father of three children under study, I really think that the law on the guidance and success of students, which we voted for, which you deployed very quickly, Madam Minister, and which fully fits into this 2019 budget, allows and will reduce false departures. I would like to take this opportunity to welcome the involvement of high school teachers, who, as part of this reform, are fully playing the map of active orientation, especially when they occupy the beautiful mission of dedicated principal teacher. I'm going to the credits for 2019. For the higher education part, i.e. the 150 programmes "Higher education and university research" and 231 "Student life", the increase is EUR 173 million. The Government is also planning to carry forward 43 million unconsumed credits this year, in 2019, bringing the total appropriations to 216 million. These appropriations will enable operators to finance wage measures, in particular the GVT – a technical old age shift – and the tenure of part of the contract staff, and to apply the accompanying measures and individualised journeys in the first cycle. University research will also benefit from an increase of $34 million in commitment authorities to the National Research Agency. The university budget is also reinforced by the appropriations of the major investment plan, which spends EUR 1.55 billion on quinquennium for higher education. This major plan is structured into two main actions: first, the "new courses at university", which promote the best consideration of the academic, geographical and social diversity of undergraduate students, and second, the "development of a world-class university", which speaks for itself. With regard to the structure of the mission's operators, first and foremost universities, the transition to expanded responsibilities and competences has become the common law of all universities. However, too many establishments remain in a fragile financial situation and the gap between the authorized employment ceiling and actual employment remains large: almost 12,000 full-time equivalents. This must lead us to pursue the search for a rebalancing between recurrent means and funding through calls for projects, since the latter has probably taken too much space. This also involves the large scale development of own resources, which need to be diversified and increased, in order to allow for a real autonomy of the institutions. For example, in 2017, universities' own resources amounted to €2.2 billion, or 16% of their operating budget. But this figure has increased by only 10 million compared to 2014 and this stagnation is worrying. The strengthening of the supply of continuing training could be a means of remedying this in part. On this point, blockages and incentives can still be removed, for example in the context of strategic dialogues that condition the allocation of resources to operators. Before I say a word about student life, I would like to plead for the facilitation of the awarding of diplomas recognized in the LMD scheme – bachelor's, master's, doctoral – for schools certified by the Ministry of Higher Education and Research and regular evaluations of the High Council for the Evaluation of Research and Higher Education, says HCERES. Of course, I am thinking of ESPIG – private higher education institutions of general interest – which fully fulfil public service tasks. Our higher education system would gain in readability, especially internationally and for a number of families, somewhat lost in the maquis of bac+3, including bachelors. Finally, with regard to student life, the amount allocated to grants on social criteria remains stable, at 2.1 billion, as well as the resources allocated to CROUS – regional centres for academic and academic works – at 480 million. The implementation of the CVEC – a contribution to student and campus life – will also allow more than 100 million to be allocated to improve student living conditions, while the price of the university restaurant ticket continues to be frozen at the start of 2019 in order not to affect the purchasing power of students. [[Applause on a few benches of the LaREM group]] In total, the 2019 budget for higher education and student life is up to the educational, scientific and simply human ambition of training our young people. In his latest book, 21 lessons for the 21st century, historian Yuval Harari states that what children are learning today will be useless by 2050 and that we do not know what the labour market will look like at that time: more than a swan song, I see it as a wonderful opportunity for the superior's institutions to position themselves increasingly in the field of lifelong learning, for which I know, Madam Minister, your sensitivity.

ParlaMint-FR_2018-11-13-O1061.u746	Mr Pierre Henriet, draftsman of the opinion of the Committee on Cultural Affairs and Education, has the floor.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-11-13-O1061.u747	I will move quickly on to the research appropriations, which have already been presented by the Minister and the Special Rapporteur, and to the adoption of which the Committee on Cultural Affairs has delivered a favourable opinion. I would just like to welcome the continuation of the research effort, with an increase of 2.2% in appropriations, in the budgetary context we know. It seems to me essential to maintain this commitment on a sustainable basis, because with about 2.2% of our GDP spent on research expenditure, we are still well below the 3% target set by Europe in 2000 and reaffirmed in 2013. I will mention in one word the question of the exploitation of research, which is one of the keys to the competitiveness of our modern economies. In a report last March, the Court of Auditors drew up a mixed assessment of the valuation tools put in place under the IAP, whether they are technology transfer acceleration companies, technological research institutes, energy transition institutes or thematic valorisation consortia. The Court points out in particular that the creation of new structures, without rationalising the existing one, has led to a form of sedimentation of the devices and to difficulties of governance of the 'ecosystem of valorisation', which has become very complex. The Court also points to initial results of these structures below expectations. The Government has taken several measures since the beginning of the year to promote and disseminate innovation, first of all by putting some of the structures I have just mentioned to an end and by carrying out evaluations. It also included measures to promote the transition between research and entrepreneurship in the PACTE bill; He has set up an innovation fund and an interdepartmental council, while introducing the Deep Tech plan, focusing on breaking innovations, essential to positioning our economy in high-tech sectors. I also welcome the increase in the resources allocated to CIFRE in this draft budget, as these conventions are a very useful instrument and appreciated by both PhD students and businesses. I come to this year's theme: the research tax credit. It is an emblematic tax system, first of all because of its cost, of the order of 6 billion euros – the first tax expenditure in our budget, after the CICE –, then because of its relative stability over time, a characteristic relatively rare in tax matters, and finally because of its place in supporting the R&D expenditure of companies. It alone accounts for two-thirds of public support for private R&D, with a total of 0.4% of GDP. The IRC is a key element in the competitiveness of the French tax environment for research and innovation activities, as all my interlocutors have pointed out. One of the strengths of the IRC is its neutrality with respect to research projects developed by companies, unlike direct subsidy schemes. The first issue of the IRC is, of course, the assessment of its impact: while a large number of reports have been conducted, they generally conclude that it is difficult to assess its effectiveness precisely, particularly for methodological reasons. There is, however, a consensus on an additionality effect of the CIR, i.e. that for each euro of CIR paid to enterprises, their R&amp;D effort would increase by an average of one euro. But it would be necessary to refine the assessments; A number of work on this subject is expected in the near future and we will have to pay attention to it, ladies and gentlemen. The second key issue is the impact of the IRC on the employment of researchers and young doctors. There are no specific statistical data on the number of researchers and young doctors supported by the IRC, although the number of researchers in companies has grown steadily since 2008, reaching 226000 in 2015. I agree with my colleague Amélie de Montchalin's concern to strengthen the assessment of the impact of the IRC on the employment of researchers; An amendment has been tabled to this effect, which will be discussed on Thursday, together with the non-attached articles. Finally, it is essential for SMEs to be able to take ownership of the CIR, even though it is these companies that are best able to develop and sustain break-up innovations. The use of rescript should therefore be further encouraged for these enterprises. It would also be useful to expand the staff of the regional research and technology delegations, DRRT, which play a role of expertise in the field of RIC in the territories, while facilitating research and innovation policies as close as possible to companies; I have tabled an amendment to that effect. [[Applause on the benches of the LaREM group]] I will conclude my remarks with a question to the minister: could you give us some insight into the future of the IRC, with a view to the adoption of the European ACCIS Directive, a common consolidated corporate tax base, and its compatibility with the proposed research schemes, such as the "super-deduction"?
ParlaMint-FR_2018-11-13-O1061.u748	Mr Philippe Berta, draftsman of the opinion of the Committee on Cultural Affairs and Education, has the floor.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-11-13-O1061.u749	This year's higher education budget is in line with the 2018 Finance Act's trajectory as it again shows an increase, in a fiscal context that remains constrained. This increase includes funding the Bachelor's Plan, a fundamental plan to improve the orientation and success of undergraduate students, creating a new scholarship to facilitate first-year student mobility, and upgrading officer careers. Over and above the review of credits for higher intelligence, I wanted to focus this year on the careers of teachers-researchers by looking for ways to better value their achievement throughout the spectrum of their missions and to provide them with more personalized professional support. Teachers-researchers have a wide range of tasks: research, pedagogy, student orientation, but also administrative responsibilities, international cooperation, dissemination of knowledge, and the exploitation of research. However, it is the quality of the research that remains the first, if not the only, criterion for the evaluation and promotion of teachers-researchers. This overvalorisation of research is coupled with insufficient professional support. There is currently no satisfactory process of recognition and accompaniment of the teacher-researcher in his professional achievement. It is evaluated only on the basis of its research activities, and on a purely voluntary basis, on the occasion of a request for promotion, promotion, transfer, bonus or thematic leave. Activities other than research are subject to little or no follow-up. This lack of accompaniment leads to difficulties and inequalities in career development. First of all, the mobility of teachers-researchers is very low, whether hierarchical, geographical or functional. In addition, inequalities in career development exist between teachers-researchers in small institutions, where research activity is often more modest, and those in larger institutions, in a often more prestigious scientific environment. This inequality is reinforced by promotion procedures, since teachers-researchers in smaller schools can only be promoted through the national channel, with the local channel closed to them. The weakness of professional accompaniment is also a factor of immobilism, but also of injustices. Of course, career monitoring was put in place in 2014 to address some of these challenges. It makes it possible to introduce a regular external look at careers, but it only very imperfectly responds to the need to better accompany the professional development of research teachers and to provide mechanisms of promotion corresponding to the reality of the profession. The evaluation must meet criteria of transparency, fairness and fairness. As far as teachers-researchers are concerned, it must respect their qualifications and independence, recognised by the Constitutional Council. Only a formative evaluation can therefore be relevant. In this regard, a number of virtuous practices for valuing educational investment should be better known and disseminated. This is the case with the preparation of teaching records detailing the methods of teaching during a given period, and the evaluation of teachings by students, when it is conducted on a voluntary basis and its results remain confidential. These evaluation mechanisms should be accompanied by increased support for teachers-researchers within their institutions and the establishment of tools dedicated to their professional development. For example, periodic and confidential milestones with the institution's human resources department could be planned every two and a half years to allow for the development of personalized advice and the provision of appropriate tools and training. A strengthening of university pedagogy services and the pedagogical training of young lecturers would also be useful, as would the development of peer-reviewing practices and third-times during which teachers-researchers could discuss their choices and difficulties in teaching. In addition, more regular and comprehensive career monitoring should lead to a fairer system for awarding promotions, leave and bonuses. It would remove the self-censorship of many agents who hesitate to seek promotion and better recognize teachers-researchers working in institutions with less research activity. These proposals could find suitable testing ground in institutions labeled IDEX and I-SITE, whose human resources are often more structured. Madam Minister, I hope that these proposals can contribute to the momentum that you have launched in favour of recognizing the educational investment of teachers-researchers. It is about the attractiveness of the profession of teacher-researcher, the place of the French university in the world, as well as the success of our students.

The hearings showed that the scheme was still too centralized and too unattractive for small and medium-sized enterprises. They can be facilitators in this area. University presidents are trying to get it, but at the same time they fear it – in a way, they often claim only seed money. I believe that an economic model can be developed without necessarily once again appealing to state funds. [[Applause on the benches of the LaREM group]] In the hope, ladies and gentlemen, that my proposals will find a positive response, I am of course expressing on behalf of the Committee on Economic Affairs a very favourable opinion on the adoption of the appropriations for the research and higher education mission.

ParlaMint-FR_2018-11-13-O1061.u756	Mrs Marie-George Buffet has the floor for the Group of the Democratic and Republican Left.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-11-13-O1061.u757	First of all, let me greet the inhabitants of French Polynesia who can follow our debates on an issue as important as higher education and research, which is not the case of French and French people in the metropolis, as well as a large number of parliamentarians... France spends only 2.2% of gross domestic product on research and development, which puts our country below the 3% target of the Lisbon strategy. This raises questions about the role of successive governments in research, in particular public research. Yet, once again, it is not the latter that is favoured in this budget that is said to be forced while the state is depriving itself of revenue with the tax credit search. Since 2008, the cost of the research tax credit has exploded. In 2017, it cost public finances no less than €6.1 billion, compared to €1.8 billion in 2007. Systemically undervalued, it is expected to reach 6.2 billion next year. As the Special Rapporteur has pointed out, its effects on the development of research and on the employment of researchers are difficult to measure. All the studies published agree on the fact that it produces little leverage on research, with one euro of CIR leading in the best case to one euro invested. In addition, 80% of the appropriations are received by large groups, which has the effect of indisputable bounties. If it can be said that a growing SME needs this tax aid to expand its R & D spending, we know that large groups have the means to make these expenditures and have an interest in doing so. In addition, the IRC, like any neutral tax expenditure of this nature – I am thinking in particular of the IEC – raises a problem in its very conception. Indeed, no criteria on the nature of the research are established: whether you are doing research and development to set up financial algorithms or research in favour of the energy transition, you benefit from the same tax credit. The State, at a minimum, should be able to make it conditional on developed research, its social or environmental usefulness. For example, between 2008 and 2016, Sanofi eliminated 4,700 positions, including 2000 of engineers and researchers, while paying almost 30 billion dividends over this period. However, in 2016, this group received 130 million research tax credits. Why is the state giving away the 130 million, which could have been allocated to the CNRS for example? The CIR thus seems to be an opportunity for some companies: they capture it by diverting it from its primary function and it becomes a tool for tax optimisation. Meanwhile, the public research budget stagnates and the CNRS continues to reduce the number of its recruitments. Here is the main pitfall of our fiscal policies. It has been years and years since our tax base was eroded by different governments. We are depriving ourselves of tens of billions of tax revenues through a whole bunch of tax exemptions that always benefit the best, whether businesses or households. At the same time, to compensate for these revenue losses, the expenditure is forced. The research illustrates this paradox perfectly. Let me quote Alain Prochiantz, a researcher in neurobiology and professor at the Collège de France: "Let us only note that depriving public research of these additional billions in its annual budget condemns our country to fall into international competition sooner or later and to lose its fifth or sixth world power in the production of knowledge. » The IRC must therefore be refocused on those companies that really need it and, above all, it must be able to finance research that is in the public interest. Public research, in particular basic and non-programmable research, also suffers from the use of the call-for-project system. This year, the National Research Agency continues to receive an increase of 33 million dollars. Yet its operation by call for projects becomes a real difficulty for many researchers. Only 14% of the proposed projects are finally funded, so scientists are losing their time and energy in seeking mortgage financing. This constraint on the freedom of researchers is a barrier to fundamental research, discovery and initiative: it derails scientific production. Similarly, the race for publications is a real danger to the quality of the research. Finally, I regret that nothing in this budget improves the living and working conditions of doctoral students and tens of thousands of underpaid individual contractors, without whom, however, the University could not function. [[Applause on the benches of the GDR and FI groups]] For all these reasons, the GDR Group will not vote for the research and higher education mission.

ParlaMint-FR_2018-11-13-O1061.u759	I am not sure that it is entirely natural to write up higher education and research, which are major areas of the future, at three o'clock in the morning. The European credit system, the adoption of a policy of quality assurance of institutions and training promote the international dimension of higher education, and that is a welcome one. I would like to mention in passing the declaration of the network of ultramarine universities, which call for the development of a research strategy, through their involvement in cooperation with their various neighbouring countries. In the field of university organisation, a dual dynamic, complementary, must be pursued: on the one hand, the affirmation of the autonomy of the institutions, which guarantees an adaptation of the offer of training to local conditions; on the other, incentives for clustering within competitiveness clusters, site contracts or collaborative research projects. It is an indispensable step in the face of the needs of time and the competition imposed by the major world organizations. It is also important that a grouping take place within the French framework, in which it must be said that one loses a little bit in the face of the proliferation of schools, universities and various bodies, which come in addition to private research, and which it is hoped do not go too far on their feet. Integrated research has also been mentioned in the European framework. We know what the cost of France's and Europe's insufficiency in the digital field is, as we know the influence, independence and employment of Ariane's success. And we can only welcome the inclusion in this budget of the 205 million to secure the development of the Ariane 6 launcher. The polarisation of French and, more broadly, European institutions is the answer to the task forces of the traditional superpowers, but also to the rise in strength of China and emerging countries. Programme 172, an instrument for the management of research, is structured around ten axes, all of which are of crucial importance. It is essential to link basic and applied research and to foster interaction with businesses. Companies, public finances and society as a whole must be able to play win-win. It is understood, for example, that the development of biofuels, offshore wind energy, the circular economy, in short, everything that converges towards the ecological and energy transition is an indispensable path towards the future, but also constitutes a considerable set of initiatives and jobs, as well as agricultural technology or health research. We know that universities are in serious deficit. We know that precariousness is rampant in the student community. We are not unaware of the criticisms that this budget has aroused, in particular the fact that it represents only 2.27% of GDP, when the target in Europe is 3%. We are aware that the CNRS and the National Institute for Research in Computer Science and Computer Science – Inria – continue to lose high-level technicians, as we know the criticisms of the Conference of University Presidents, trade unions and student unions. But we also take note of the efforts made, knowing that they will have to be continued and amplified in the future. [[Smile]] I wish you a good and pleasant night.

For the structuring of higher education and research institutions in the territory, you propose the experimentation of new forms of grouping. For higher education and student life, the course and trajectory are preserved. For the research part, our objectives were to maintain the excellence of French research, to ensure the competitiveness of our economy through breaking innovation and to strengthen France's presence in European projects, particularly in coordination tasks. With regard to breaking innovation, the aim was to remove the obstacles to recovery. In the law for the growth and transformation of enterprises – PACTE – the reform of the single agent system offers the possibility of sharing the time of the researcher between the company and the laboratory. In this finance bill for 2019, the budget for industrial research training agreements increases by 15%. In addition, an Innovation and Industry Fund and an Innovation Council have been established. Research appropriations are increasing by 332 million. This increase is allocated to the RDA to increase the selection rate, salary measures, artificial intelligence plan and international projects. To these appropriations, we must add those of the mission "Investments for the future", in particular for the exploitation of research. The La République en Marche group will therefore vote for the "Research and Higher Education" mission.

As such, we would like to acknowledge the success of the new Pathup orientation platform, which, for the first year of application, has been successful. Of course, specific cases of difficulty have been raised during our debates in committee: they will help us to improve the arrangements even further, as of next year. The 150 programme for higher education and university research saw several spreads realized, such as the significant increase in action 01 "Initial and continuing training of the bachelor's degree" of more than 3% to support an important reform brought about by the ORE law: the accompaniment of students who have received a "yes, if" in Parcoursup. Overall, funding for this program increases by 1.23% to reflect changing student demographics. The Minister committed herself and 25,000 places were created in the 2018 school year. At least 7,000 will be for the 2019 school year. In addition, the 2018 school year saw the introduction of the "tailor-made" license, which should allow for greater flexibility in the student journey. This effort for the bachelor's level is also supported for the master's and doctoral levels. Other measures of the ORE Act have their application to the 231 "Student Life" program. The wider application of the student plan has direct and indisputable effects which we must stress and which go in the direction of improving the living conditions of students: the connection to the general social security system makes it possible to simplify the procedures for students and effectively leads to the abolition of the student social security contribution of 217 euros, which is far from negligible in a student's budget. A student and campus life contribution of €90 per year has been created to improve sports and cultural infrastructure for students. Finally, the free rental guarantee scheme, VISALE, was extended to students aged 18 to 30 for an amount of 600 euros outside Île-de-France and 800 euros for Île-de-France. In total, as a result of better account being taken of the international dimension of the student journey, the gains in purchasing power, the improvement of student life, the best management of health expenditure, the rental guarantee, this majority has worked, through concrete measures, to improve higher education. As for the part devoted to research, budgets are strengthened for the various programmes, allowing France to rank fifth among OECD member countries, ahead of the United Kingdom, Spain or Italy, and even second if investment in public research is taken into account. Our researchers are recognized throughout the world for their excellence and it is important that their results can be known to everyone and disseminated to as many people as possible through actions to promote scientific culture. That is the point of Mr Philippe Berta's amendment, which was voted on in committee. Finally, we welcome the fact that many programmes are developing at European level, such as space research, and we hope that these steps will be stepped up. ParlaMint-FR_2018-11-13-O1061.u770	I call the appropriations of the mission "Research and higher education", entered in state B. On these appropriations, I have several amendments before me. I call Mr Michel Larive to support Amendment No. 1894.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-11-13-O1061.u771	It aims to transfer funds from the National Research Agency to the National Centre for Scientific Research. The RNA is an additional administrative step that complicates and slows down the scientific process. It is more than necessary to give priority to the efficiency of French research, by financially supporting operators such as the CNRS, whose only vocation is scientific production. The CNRS was created after the Liberation and operated independently until 2005, when the ANR was created. For more than fifty years, he has conducted research programs that have led to major discoveries, rewarded with the highest international distinctions. While, in the spirit of the founding fathers, the CNRS was originally a structure that could allocate funds for research programmes itself, this public institution subsequently suffered a slow disconnection from its prerogatives, some of which, such as the evaluation and allocation of funds between research programmes, were entrusted to the ANR. The observation is as follows: either before the creation of the ANR, the CNRS and the other operators were not doing their job well, or it is considered that the CNRS made its greatest discoveries before 2005 and, therefore, that an additional administrative level, such as the ANR, is not really useful. It also seems important to us to ask ourselves what kind of governance we want for public research. At the NRA, the members of the research program selection committees are appointed. At the CNRS, the members were elected, which allowed for respect for the plurality of opinions of the scientific community. With the ANR, the nomination was preferred to the election. Consequently, the amendment aims to abound the new programme "National Centre for Scientific Research" from the appropriations of Action 02 "National Research Agency", as well as from the appropriations which were automatically transferred to the CNRS in Action 01 of Programme 172.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-11-13-O1061.u772	What is the opinion of the committee?

ParlaMint-FR_2018-11-13-O1061.u804	I call Mr Michel Larive to support Amendment No. 1718.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-11-13-O1061.u805	Sputnik, the first man-launched artificial satellite in space on October 4, 1957, revolutionized the world. Since then, space missions have multiplied, without concern for the fate of objects left in space, given the little impact on human life; Indeed, the smallest elements disintegrate into the atmosphere before touching the ground, like any space object. Since Sputnik, more than 8500 launches have been made, most of which have not exceeded the Earth's gravitational influence zone. According to NASA figures, between 1957 and 2006, the number of space objects in Earth orbit grew approximately linearly, with an increase of about two hundred and twenty new objects per year. The number of space wastes has increased dramatically on two occasions: on 11 January 2007, China destroyed its former meteorological satellite, which was no longer in service, in an anti-satellite operation to test remote destruction capability. The satellite was destroyed, but its fragments were dispersed, generating 2000 new debris, doubling their number at once; On 10 February 2009, the first collision took place between two satellites, Iridium 33, weighing 560 kilograms and Kosmos-2251, a Russian military telecommunications satellite that was out of service and weighed 900 kilograms. This collision generated 2000 new debris. Currently, the combined problems of space waste and satellites already in orbit are truly forcing launches and forcing permanent satellite monitoring to avoid collisions. The international space station – ISS – often has to manoeuvre to avoid damage from large debris. In these circumstances, the issue of low-orbit remediation arises, if we are to continue to rely on space technology in the future. Indeed, many of our daily actions go through space, even GPS geolocation, not to mention the implications in the telecommunications and weather and military forecasting sectors. This amendment aims to make France a pioneer nation in this great task of human interest, the clean-up of the low orbit.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-11-13-O1061.u806	What is the opinion of the committee?
ParlaMint-FR_2018-11-13-O1061.u807	There are international rules on space debris, which I will not quote here, but which I would be happy to share with you. The European Space Agency has developed a clean space programme on the subject, and many technologies are still being studied and tested to find the best way to recover or neutralize space debris. From a budgetary point of view, France will increase its contribution to the European Space Agency by €210 million in 2019. We are investing in an ambitious space policy. The subject of debris is of course covered by this commitment and investment. If you did not withdraw your amendment, I would express an opinion against its adoption.

ParlaMint-FR_2018-11-13-O1061.u824	It aims to create a budget line entitled "Research for international cooperation for interplanetary manned flight". The International Space Station – ISS – is the largest artificial space object manufactured by humanity and the only one permanently occupied by a crew of six astronauts, who relayed for missions of three to six months. Launched by NASA in 1983, the project was carried out only through international cooperation, bringing together Russian, European, Japanese and Canadian space agencies. It was finally completed in 2011. Astronaut Thomas Pesquet's mission popularized it to the French public, as well as the scientific missions carried out there. Space is not, by vocation, a place of confrontation, on the contrary – even though it has often been, especially during the cold war. The International Space Station is the very example demonstrating that international cooperation is needed to achieve the level of technology needed to carry out projects. The United States, the great power it was, had to wait for the decisive contribution of the Russians to complete the construction of the station. At present, the whole world depends on Soviet technology to join the ISS. All major space projects are the result of international cooperation between space powers. Space is the very place where cooperation is needed, where human solidarity transcends any consideration of nationality. Faced with the radical otherness of space, the general human interest is affirmed as a desirable and necessary horizon.

ParlaMint-FR_2018-11-13-O1061.u859	The minister has the floor.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-11-13-O1061.u860	Ladies and gentlemen, you are about to consider several amendments on one and the same subject, which is mobilising us all. I would therefore like to take a moment to lay some milestones in the debate that is about to take place. Every year, around 1,700 cases of cancer are detected in children and adolescents under the age of 15. They cause 500 deaths a year. Each of these deaths is an absolute tragedy that strikes and affects us all. There are no words to express the lives of families who see a child disappear. To accompany them, know him, mark him forever. Personally, I have accompanied, as part of my research, families of children with genetic abnormalities leading to their death after horrific suffering. I'll never forget it. All of us, on all benches, have only this one and the same goal of reducing pediatric cancers. In a few moments, I know we will have the opportunity to make strong decisions together to achieve this. But I also wanted to tell you that in the face of this pain, in the face of this injustice – because there is nothing more incomprehensible than to see a child disappear – there would be something derisory to want to get lost in the numbers. What we want to do is the maximum, that is, first of all, to adopt the right strategy, the one that will produce results, the one that will actually reduce this disease and the mortality it causes. That's why I want to take a moment to tell you where we are in this fight. The rates of cure of childhood cancers are now close to 80%; This is an increase of 20% over the last 15 years. This progress is obviously due to the mobilization of all scientists and doctors, supported by society as a whole, particularly in the context of successive cancer plans. The challenge today for children's cancers as well as for a number of others is to treat those who resist the therapeutic strategies built in recent years. I am thinking of certain forms of brain stem tumours, or of certain sarcomas: so much progress has been made in treating leukemia, as much for these particular forms we are still very poor. However, they affect children in particular. These difficulties do not come, as I have sometimes heard, from chronic underfunding – but I do not want to start tonight in accounting for misfortune, it would make no sense. The means of the cancer plan have been there for years, and they benefit greatly from research on paediatric cancers. Each year, the National Cancer Institute – INCa – funds 64 million euros in cancer research; Just over 8 million are specifically dedicated to paediatric cancers. Of course, these amounts do not include payroll or laboratory funding by universities or research organizations. It is risky, for a reason that many of you will understand and that your Special Rapporteur knows well, to try to give a consolidated figure of expenditure: today we do not have a common information system for research actors that would allow the efforts undertaken by each institution to be combined. This evening you will have the opportunity to remedy this, by including in the law this obligation for all public research. But beyond that, these consolidated figures are in any case difficult to establish. The important thing is to share a clear vision of what we can expect from research. The reality is, there's not one, there's cancer. Some mechanisms are common to them; not all. If we want to defeat the most resistant cancers, we must above all change our approach and understand what makes them unique, in order to imagine new and innovative strategies. To do this, we need all the basic research. Fighting pediatric cancers, it is the oncologists who say so, it is the researchers of the Curie Institute who say so, it is increasing the knowledge of the fundamental mechanisms, especially embryonic development – because the cancers of the child are mostly developmental anomalies, which makes their peculiarity. It also proposes new methods of study and analysis, new methods of imaging and sequencing, new cellular or animal models. It's still thinking of new therapeutic approaches, such as proton therapy, immunotherapy, laser surgery... It is finally working at European level, because these paediatric cancers are all rare diseases; But the formation of cohorts is necessary to ask the right questions, and therefore the right diagnoses. I will take only one example here: that of infiltrating tumors in the brain stem. We do not yet know what discipline the solution will come from: improved techniques of radiosurgery, resulting from research in physics? Developmental biology, by better characterization of these tissues? From the analysis of the development of the nervous system, affected by this cancer? Even yesterday, I was discussing with Professor Delattre and his team, who have been working on these issues for years: researchers also evoke artificial intelligence, big data; They talk about attracting talent from the scientific world from other disciplines, because that's where the stakes are. That is why the Government wants to propose an amendment No. 2513, which will allow 5 million euros to be spent in 2019 on research aimed at reducing cancers in children. These budgetary appropriations will of course become recurrent. This year, $2 million will come from the redeployment of research investments, and $3 million will be redeployed from another program. Therefore, we do not propose to allocate these 5 million euros for any account balance, but to make them a long-term commitment, and shared. The long-term funding strategy for the most treatment-resistant cancers and paediatric cancers is in the context of the follow-up to the cancer plan. This will concern both basic research and translational research, or clinical trials under the programmes concerned, which do not depend on this task, as well as the care and support of care. This is the framework within which Agnès Buzyn and I can calibrate our country's multi-year effort. Again, research on paediatric cancers in particular also feeds research on all other cancers: these two fields, although different, are not leakproof. Every knowledge acquired about pediatric cancer benefits the entire research, as inversely every knowledge acquired in the field of developmental biology or every progress in the development of a laser at Polytechnic can provide therapeutic solutions for childhood cancers. This work will of course be carried out in close consultation with the National Assembly. You will be involved in this reflection and we will be able to make the necessary milestones together to build consensus on this issue. We must make it a common cause. That is why I will ask the authors of the various amendments tabled this evening on this subject to withdraw them for the benefit of the Government. Please, let's not get into trouble with numbers when we share a common goal. Let us pass a unique message to children and their families: each one in our place, each in our role, we are at work; They can count on us. That is why I would like to conclude by thanking your House for dealing with this essential issue. Mr President of the Committee on Finance, the Special Rapporteur, this case has found in you excellent lawyers. I also welcome the authors of the amendments, Mrs Trastour-Isnart, Mrs Descamps, Mr Saddier, Mr Brun, Mr Diard, Mr Perrut and, of course, Mr Woerth. Together, you laid, we laid the foundations for a common mobilization at the service of children and their families. [[Applause on the benches of the LaREM and MODEM groups]] It is in this spirit that I invite you to vote for the amendment that the Government will present to you, following parliamentary work, that I believe to be exemplary and fruitful.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-11-13-O1061.u861	Mr President, the Committee on Finance, the General Economy and Budgetary Control has the floor.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-11-13-O1061.u862	It is a question that brings us all together: there is obviously no trial of intent on such a sensitive and painful subject.
