ParlaMint-FR_2018-11-13-O1061.u885	But why five million?
ParlaMint-FR_2018-11-13-O1061.u886	We have the chance to have the INCa, which centralizes all the funding dedicated to cancer research, which will also guarantee the dipping of the money and a follow-up of its use, to which we must associate parents, families, parliamentarians and others – this is an issue that we will have to dig into. The INCa brings together all the specific funding devoted to the fight against cancer, excluding salaries and recurrent funding of laboratory research in general. Of its budget, 12% are specifically directed towards paediatric cancers, i.e. €8 million. Not a single project assessed as a "true" project, combining basic, translational and clinical research as well as a trial, is not funded by INCa. Adding €5 million to this €8 million means providing funding for quality projects. Adding 18 million euros would be complicated. It would be necessary to allocate only 5 to INCa, thus increasing to 13 million for projects evaluated positively, as we expect – which is already huge. The rest would be devoted to research in general. But I don't know how to turn to pediatric cancers the money goes to "research in general"! The EUR 5 million we are proposing, which is intended for basic research – remember that they are going to the 172 programme – seem a correct and sensible amount in relation to the projects currently funded in the field of basic research. The subject is eminently interdepartmental, as I said, but essentially between the Ministry of Health and the Ministry of Research. The cancer plan that Agnès Buzyn and I must prepare for the year 2020 must present a vision of the funding of basic laboratory research, translational research, clinical research, but also of the formation of cohorts. It is therefore €6 million, through the investment programme for the future, to finance cohorts of sick children. The cohorts formed will sometimes be interviewed for pediatric cancers related to genetic abnormalities, and sometimes for rare genetic diseases that cause cancers. I therefore do not consider this EUR 6 million to be specific funding for paediatric cancers. It is within the framework of the future cancer plan that we will have to work the whole chain. I understand that the estimate made by doctors, hospitals, pediatric oncology centres and families is in this order of magnitude, but precisely because it includes funding for cohorts and clinical trials, support for care or foster care for families, which are absolutely not part of Program 172. These are the reasons for this recurring €5 million choice, which is accompanied by the commitment to work under the cancer plan on cancers for which there is no effective therapy. Indeed, the effectiveness of treatments has increased as we mastered chemotherapy. All that can be treated with chemotherapy or radiation therapy is now: you can still improve treatments or reduce sequelae, but it is treated. But remain 20% for which we do not manage to increase treatment and remission rates, because we do not understand what is happening. Chemotherapy, radiation therapy doesn't work. Maybe the solution will come from lasers, or chemistry... When we reach a level in therapeutic treatments, we've finished treating everything we know, everything we've understood. That is why it is so important to have as many disciplines as possible work together to find another way to treat what is now resistant to chemotherapy and radiation therapy. It is a joint fight with which families must be involved. It's a fight in which you have to talk to them a lot. The emotional load is enormous, but when they suffer the worst, the most important thing for them is to know that they may contribute to what the worst does not happen to others. We must succeed in uniting this solidarity of families within associations, as was the case for rare diseases: associations that had been created around a terrible case have worked together, have structured themselves and today fully participate in the co-construction of the rare diseases plan with the ministries responsible for research and health. [[Applause on the benches of the LaREM group and some benches of the LR group]] In short, yes, these are funding for paediatric cancers. Yes, we will be able to dart them and follow them, because INCa exists. Yes, it is essential to define the right calibration for Program 172, but also to be able to go further next year as part of the next cancer plan. Yes, we will work with patient families, researchers, doctors and, of course, parliamentarians because this subject must concern us all.

ParlaMint-FR_2018-11-13-O1061.u1021	The aim of this amendment, on nuclear issues, is to request the submission of a report detailing all the budgetary allocations allocated to security and safety policy. Office of the Atomic Energy Commissioner – CEA –, Institute for Radiation Protection and Nuclear Safety – IRSN –, Nuclear Safety Authority – ASN –, High Committee for Transparency and Information on Nuclear Security, Local Information Commissions – CLI – and National Association of Local Information Committees and Commissions – ANCCLI: All these endowments contribute to the same objective. Today, however, we do not have a transversal vision. The French would benefit greatly from having a good visibility of the public resources we devote to these very important issues.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-11-13-O1061.u1022	What is the Government's opinion?
ParlaMint-FR_2018-11-13-O1061.u1023	Each year, during the preparation of the initial finance bill, the information necessary to inform Parliament is organised in the annual performance projects – PAP – for forecasting and annual performance reports – RAP – for the implementation of a given budget programme. Each lead program tracks the credits associated with its operator(s), whose activity is related to nuclear safety or security. It is true that it is the organization that controls this mechanism, the reference being the PAP or the RAP of the lead program. A new budget document would add a new stratum to a set of documents, the presentation of which is already, at the very least, complex for a non-initiated person. I therefore ask that this amendment be withdrawn. Otherwise, the opinion will be unfavourable.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-11-13-O1061.u1024	The Special Rapporteur has the floor.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-11-13-O1061.u1025	Minister, I would like to be able to work on this with your teams so that, if this consolidation does not take the form of a report prepared by the Government, we can, together with the formidable National Assembly teams, produce this document and make it available to the citizens concerned. Perhaps this is not a lot of work. If that's the case, that's good. If it's a little more, we can talk about it again. Thank you and I withdraw my amendment.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-11-13-O1061.u1026	The Special Rapporteur has the floor to support Amendment No. 993.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-11-13-O1061.u1027	This amendment could enable us all to be much more educated and informed in advance of the debate on multi-annual energy programming. I am defending him with my colleague Émilie Cariou, a member of the Parliamentary Office for the Evaluation of Scientific and Technical Options (OPECST), who seeks to gain greater visibility of the cost-security arbitration of the various technical options that exist, notably for the landfill and management of nuclear waste. In 2016, Ségolène Royal estimated the cost of the Cigeo Industrial Geological Storage Centre project at 25 billion euros. At the same time, the National Agency for Radioactive Waste Management – ANDRA – was talking about €34.5 billion. In 2013, the project cost was estimated at $16 billion and, in 2014, the range was estimated to be between 15, 34, 39 and 43. This means that we lack really in-depth expertise on the options differences that exist. It is not a question of pushing one or the other by dogma or by any religion, but of enlightening the debate on the choices made and understanding their respective advantages and disadvantages, and of appreciating the cost-safe benefit. The aim is to find for the French the solution which, if it is very expensive, is also the safest – and it might be interesting that, if it is a little cheaper, it is just as safe. Madam Minister, the issue of nuclear power will be of importance to the French, because waste is a big issue. To date, the public debate has not been informed. We lack the expertise and soliciting experts would be very good news.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-11-13-O1061.u1028	What is the Government's opinion?
ParlaMint-FR_2018-11-13-O1061.u1029	The Environmental Code provides that the national plan for the management of radioactive materials and waste shall include an estimate of the costs of the management of spent fuel and waste, indicating the assumptions that this estimate has been made. This national management plan is updated every three years and each edition, before its adoption, is forwarded to OPECST. The cost elements associated with the management of radioactive waste can therefore already be examined. As far as the Cigeo project is concerned, the Order of 15 January 2016 on the costs of implementing long-term radioactive waste management solutions estimated the cost of the project at EUR 25 billion for the period 2016-2156. This evaluation will be updated on a regular basis and at least at the major stages of the project. I therefore ask that this amendment be withdrawn. Otherwise, I'll give you an unfavorable opinion.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-11-13-O1061.u1030	Mrs Special Rapporteur, do you maintain your amendment?
ParlaMint-FR_2018-11-13-O1061.u1031	Here too, I would ask that my colleague Émilie Cariou and I can meet your teams and also take stock with the Ministry of Ecological and Solidarity Transition. Since you confirm that it belongs to François de Rugy, we will see him. Often, in public debates, in this House, we defend evaluation and access to data. If we want science to enter into public policy debates – and I think you agree with that – the more numbers and facts we will have, the more we will be able to get out of what is sometimes a fantasy and sometimes a misinformation that some people voluntarily or unintentionally feed. I therefore withdraw my amendment, but know that my colleague and I will go knock at the doors to get answers.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-11-13-O1061.u1032	The Special Rapporteur has the floor to support Amendment No. 1861.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-11-13-O1061.u1033	Madam Minister, I think I will have a better success with this amendment than with the previous ones. The SI Lab, an information system that will consolidate data from all universities, operators and European programmes – in a nutshell, everything that animated us earlier this evening – is not an evil project, but an essential one. It is about giving you the means to carry out this project, with a convergence between universities and research organisations which, hopefully, will be really effective by the end of the quinquennium. This project already has too long years behind it and it is time for it to become a reality. I bring this amendment with Danièle Hérin, but also, I am sure, and at least in thought, with Patrick Hetzel, with whom we conducted this assessment and monitoring mission.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-11-13-O1061.u1034	Mrs Pau-Langevin has the floor to support Amendment No. 1869.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-11-13-O1061.u1035	With this amendment, we want to raise the question of the conditions for awarding scholarships according to merit. To obtain them, you must now have obtained a "very good" mention at the baccalaureate, collect a scholarship on social criteria and enter the allocation quota of the academy. Thus, there is an injustice because if a large number of young people in an academy have obtained a "very good" mark, some may not be awarded a scholarship, the number of these being fixed for each academy. We therefore call for a review of these criteria. To this end, we propose the submission of a report on the conditions for awarding these merit grants.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-11-13-O1061.u1036	What is the opinion of the committee?
ParlaMint-FR_2018-11-13-O1061.u1037	The total number of requests compared to the number of bids met is not detailed in the budget documents.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-11-13-O1061.u1038	The obligations are different according to the academies!
ParlaMint-FR_2018-11-13-O1061.u1039	Rather than asking for a specific report on the subject, it is preferable for the Minister to undertake to publish this information as part of the annual performance report, so that it can be widely accessible.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-11-13-O1061.u1040	What is the Government's opinion?
ParlaMint-FR_2018-11-13-O1061.u1041	There is no academic quota. All students who meet the conditions for the award of merit assistance set out in the annual circular on the terms and conditions for the award of higher education grants – social criteria, merit aid and assistance for international mobility – are eligible for this assistance. The Government's opinion was therefore unfavourable, since merit assistance was awarded to any student receiving a higher education grant on the basis of social criteria or an annual allowance granted under the specific aid scheme, who had a "very good" mark at the last session of the baccalaureate, and enrolled in a qualifying training course.
ParlaMint-FR_2018-11-13-O1061.u1042	[[The Minister, as well as Ms. and Ms. and Ms. Members get up and applaud]] Madam Minister, ladies and gentlemen, I think I can ask you for a moment of attention, because I am sure that I am the interpreter of the national representation by paying tribute, at this time, to all the officials of the National Assembly, whom I invite you to applaud and to which I wish to express our unanimous gratitude. Further discussion was postponed until the next sitting.
